Owner: ML platform on-call. Opened 2026-03-04, updated 2026-03-05.\n\n## What happened\n- `churn-hgb-v3` is trained on the feature table that the offline pipeline builds (`churn/`, v3 in `CHANGELOG.md`).\n  Built from this brand's extract (`data/`, the warehouse at 2026-03-05T00:00:00Z), that table gives\n  **offline validation AUC 0.805** in `python -m churn.train` (trained on the predictions before 2025-09-01,\n  validated on those of 2025-09-01 to 2025-11-30).\n- The model went live on 2026-01-01. The live scoring service computes the features of each request itself, from the\n  warehouse at the moment of the request (`live/features.py`). On the 3,415 predictions of January 2026, whose 30-day\n  outcomes have all landed in `labels`: **live AUC 0.656**.\n- The model and its hyperparameters did not change between validation and launch.\n\n## Tried so far\n- Retrained with much stronger regularization and shallower trees (`--l2 50 --max-depth 2`): offline AUC 0.813. The\n  offline number did not fall, so this is not ordinary overfitting.\n- Compared the live service with `live/features.py` by hand on 200 January requests: the service computes exactly what\n  its queries say.\n- Pulled a random sample of about 10% of the requests of January 2026, with the values the service computed:\n  `logs/live_2026-01_sample.jsonl`, 365 requests, one JSON object per line with `customer_id`, `as_of`, `trigger`, the\n  11 `features` and the model's `score`.\n\n## Next step (model team)\n`churn-hgb-v4` is to be trained on a feature table that matches what the live service computed for every past\nprediction. Every feature whose live value can be matched for every prediction is kept; any feature whose live value\ncannot be matched is dropped from v4.\n# Changelog: the offline feature pipeline (`churn/`)\n\n## v3 (2025-12-19): the training table of churn-hgb-v3\nFixes INC-2041: in four places, v2 used events stamped at or after the prediction time `t`.\n- `windows.py`: the order, spend and ticket windows are `[t - W days, t)` on the exact event timestamps. v2 summed\n  whole UTC days, the day of `t` included, so events later on the prediction day were counted.\n- `status.py`: the latest snapshot dated before `date(t)`. A snapshot dated D records the status at the end of day D;\n  v2 took the customer's latest snapshot in the extract, which is often dated after `t`.\n- `impute.py`: a missing `app_minutes_7d` takes the customer's most recent earlier reading only. v2 filled backward\n  first, from a later reading.\n- `encoding.py`: `te_district` counts only labels whose 30-day window had closed before `t` (`as_of + 30 days < t`),\n  for the district and for the prior alike. v2 used every label in the extract, the prediction's own and later ones\n  included.\n\nAlso in v3:\n- `attributes.py` (new): `plan` and `days_since_last_login` from the customer record, the two features of the live\n  service that v2 did not build. A last login at or after `t` is ignored (the feature is null then).\n- `extract.py` loads the seven tables described in `docs/WAREHOUSE.md`.\n- `train.py`: every column of the feature table other than `customer_id` and `as_of` is a model input, and string\n  columns are one-hot encoded (v2 encoded `status` only); a column that holds no value at all is skipped.\n# Warehouse tables (churn scoring)\n\nWritten 2025-02-14 by the data platform for the warehouse and its nightly loaders as they were on that date, and not\nupdated since. The scheduler's own records of every job run and transaction, later changes included, are in each\nextract's `_ops/` folder (below).\n\nThe seven tables below are the warehouse tables that the live scoring service (`live/features.py`) and the churn\npipeline (`churn/`) read. This page says what each table holds and which system writes it. Every brand of the company\nruns the same warehouse and jobs; each brand's job runs, failures and changes are its own.\n\n## Reading the warehouse\n- A query that starts at a moment t sees exactly the rows and values committed before t: a row committed at or after\n  t is not seen, and a value overwritten at or after t is seen as it was before the overwrite, whatever the date of\n  the event the row records.\n- A query reads each table by the name it has when the query starts.\n- `_loaded_at` is the commit time of the load that wrote the row; in `orders`, `_load_job` names that load.\n- An extract is a copy of every table as the warehouse held it at one moment: the rows and values committed before\n  that moment, with the `_ops/` records written before it.\n- Timestamps are UTC, written as ISO 8601 with a `Z` suffix and whole seconds (`2025-02-04T06:00:00Z`);\n  `snapshot_date` is a UTC date (`YYYY-MM-DD`); amounts are integer cents. Columns whose names start with `_` are\n  written by the loaders, not by the source systems.\n\n## The scheduler's records (`_ops/`)\n- `_ops/scheduler.log` has one UTC event per line, `<time> <job> run=<run> <event>`: each task's start\n  (`state=running`) and end (`state=success` or `state=failed`), and between them the lines the task writes, such as\n  the rows it selected or copied, the transaction it sent (`sent txn <n> to commit`), the statements it ran and the\n  checks it made.\n- `_ops/commits.jsonl` has one JSON object per transaction a scheduled job sent: `txn`, `at`, `outcome` (`commit` or\n  `rollback`), `table` and `rows` (the table the transaction writes rows into and how many; null for schema\n  statements).\n- `_ops/commits.jsonl` is the commit log of the scheduled jobs: a transaction is committed if and only if its outcome\n  is `commit`, and it is committed at its `at`; the scheduler's task states describe tasks, not commits.\n- Change data capture (`tickets`), the sign-up service and the scoring service write without the scheduler and are not\n  in `_ops/`.\n\n## Tables\n\n### `customers`\n`customer_id, signup_at, plan, postcode_district, last_login_at, _updated_at`: one row per customer.\n- The sign-up service writes the row at `signup_at`: the plan chosen at sign-up (`basic`, `plus` or `family`), the\n  postcode district, an empty `last_login_at`, and `_updated_at` = `signup_at`. `signup_at` and `postcode_district`\n  never change.\n- `crm_sync` reads the CRM at its start. For each customer whose plan or last login in the CRM (the latest login to\n  the app or website before the sync's start) differs from the row, it overwrites `plan` and `last_login_at` with the\n  CRM's values and sets `_updated_at` to the sync's commit time.\n- Only plan changes are logged (`plan_changes`); no history of `last_login_at` is kept.\n\n### `plan_changes`\n`customer_id, changed_at, old_plan, new_plan, _loaded_at`: one row per plan change that `crm_sync` has applied. Each\nsync writes one row for every change made in the CRM before its start that no earlier sync applied; `changed_at` is\nwhen the customer made the change in the CRM, `_loaded_at` the commit of the sync that applied it. A customer's rows\nare every change applied since sign-up, so the earliest row's `old_plan` is the plan chosen at sign-up. When one sync\napplies several changes of a customer, the plan after that sync is the `new_plan` of the latest of them (by\n`changed_at`), the CRM's plan at the sync's start.\n\n### `scoring_points`\n`customer_id, as_of, trigger, app_minutes_7d`: one row per scoring request, written by the scoring service at the\nmoment of the request, `as_of`. `trigger` is `batch` (the 06:00 run, which scores a customer every 14 days, on the\ncustomer's billing weekday) or `ticket` (a request the support desk sends when a ticket is opened, for some of the\ntickets, at the ticket's `opened_at`). `app_minutes_7d` is the product-analytics reading that came with the request (the customer's minutes in\nthe app over the last 7 days), empty when the request came without one (analytics export outages, devices offline).\n`(customer_id, as_of)` is unique.\n\n### `orders`\n`order_id, customer_id, ordered_at, kind, amount_cents, _load_job, _loaded_at`. `kind` is `box` (the weekly box,\ncharged by billing at 05:30 on the customer's billing weekday; `ordered_at` is the charge) or `addon` (an extra item,\nordered at any time). Each order exists in the order system, which the loaders read, from its `ordered_at` on.\nThe nightly `legacy_orders` job writes the table: each run selects orders from the order system and sends them to\n`orders` in one transaction.\n\n### `tickets`\n`ticket_id, customer_id, opened_at, topic, _loaded_at`: support tickets, written by change data capture from the\nsupport desk 1 to 30 seconds after `opened_at`.\n\n### `status_snapshots`\n`customer_id, snapshot_date, status, _loaded_at`: the billing system's account status (`active`, `paused`,\n`payment_failed` or `cancelled`) at the end of day `snapshot_date`. Billing records a row for a customer on the\nsign-up day, on every day the status changed, and on the first day of every month, up to the cancellation (a\n`cancelled` row is the customer's last); there is at most one row per customer and date.\n`billing_export` writes each day's partition (all the rows dated D) in one transaction.\n\n### `labels`\n`customer_id, as_of, churned_30d, _loaded_at`: the outcome of a prediction (`customer_id` and `as_of` as in\n`scoring_points`): `churned_30d` is 1 if the customer cancelled in `(as_of, as_of + 30 days]`, else 0. The outcome\nbecomes known at the cancellation, or when the 30 days have passed without one.\n`label_job` writes, in one transaction per run, a row for each prediction whose outcome became known before the run\nstarted and that has no row yet.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"27e1e3a7-82c5-4dfa-8d6a-ade10bab3797","uuid":"34899d5a-7d27-4011-8e20-94b916c1ec54","timestamp":"2026-11-01T02:42:35.252Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"./CHANGELOG.md\n./INCIDENT.md\n./README.md\n./churn/__init__.py\n./churn/attributes.py\n./churn/build_features.py\n./churn/encoding.py\n./churn/extract.py\n./churn/impute.py\n./churn/status.py\n./churn/train.py\n./churn/windows.py\n./data/customers.csv\n./data/labels.csv\n./data/orders.csv\n./data/plan_changes.csv\n./data/scoring_points.csv\n./data/status_snapshots.csv\n./data/tickets.csv\n./docs/WAREHOUSE.md\n./live/features.py\n./logs/live_2026-01_sample.jsonl\n./data/_ops/commits.jsonl\n./data/_ops/scheduler.log\n# INC-2077: churn-hgb-v3 scores far worse live than in validation\n\nStatus: open. Each order exists in the order system, which the loaders read, from its `ordered_at` on.\nThe nightly `legacy_orders` job writes the table: each run selects orders from the order system and sends them to\n`orders` in one transaction.\n\n### `tickets`\n`ticket_id, customer_id, opened_at, topic, _loaded_at`: support tickets, written by change data capture from the\nsupport desk 1 to 30 seconds after `opened_at`.\n\n### `status_snapshots`\n`customer_id, snapshot_date, status, _loaded_at`: the billing system's account status (`active`, `paused`,\n`payment_failed` or `cancelled`) at the end of day `snapshot_date`. Billing records a row for a customer on the\nsign-up day, on every day the status changed, and on the first day of every month, up to the cancellation (a\n`cancelled` row is the customer's last); there is at most one row per customer and date.\n`billing_export` writes each day's partition (all the rows dated D) in one transaction.\n\n### `labels`\n`customer_id, as_of, churned_30d, _loaded_at`: the outcome of a prediction (`customer_id` and `as_of` as in\n`scoring_points`): `churned_30d` is 1 if the customer cancelled in `(as_of, as_of + 30 days]`, else 0. The outcome\nbecomes known at the cancellation, or when the 30 days have passed without one.\n`label_job` writes, in one transaction per run, a row for each prediction whose outcome became known before the run\nstarted and that has no row yet.","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01E1GwQA4yJEhMAdBpzwF6vX","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

COUNT(*)\n        FROM orders\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n          AND ordered_at >= :now - INTERVAL '7 days'\n          AND ordered_at < :now\n    \"\"\",\n    # orders in the 30 days before the request\n    \"orders_30d\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT COUNT(*)\n        FROM orders\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n          AND ordered_at >= :now - INTERVAL '30 days'\n          AND ordered_at < :now\n    \"\"\",\n    # amount of those orders, in cents (0 when there are none)\n    \"spend_30d_cents\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT COALESCE(SUM(amount_cents), 0)\n        FROM orders\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n          AND ordered_at >= :now - INTERVAL '30 days'\n          AND ordered_at < :now\n    \"\"\",\n    # support tickets opened in the 30 days before the request\n    \"tickets_30d\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT COUNT(*)\n        FROM tickets\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n          AND opened_at >= :now - INTERVAL '30 days'\n          AND opened_at < :now\n    \"\"\",\n    # days since the latest order before the request (NULL when there is none)\n    \"days_since_last_order\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (:now - MAX(ordered_at))) / 86400.0\n        FROM orders\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n          AND ordered_at < :now\n    \"\"\",\n    # days since sign-up\n    \"tenure_days\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (:now - signup_at)) / 86400.0\n        FROM customers\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n    \"\"\",\n    # account status of the latest snapshot (no row: NULL)\n    \"status\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT status\n        FROM status_snapshots\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n        ORDER BY snapshot_date DESC\n        LIMIT 1\n    \"\"\",\n    # subscription plan on the customer record\n    \"plan\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT plan\n        FROM customers\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n    \"\"\",\n    # days since the last login on the customer record (NULL when the record has none)\n    \"days_since_last_login\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM (:now - last_login_at)) / 86400.0\n        FROM customers\n        WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n    \"\"\",\n    # app minutes over the last 7 days: the request's own reading, else the reading of the customer's latest earlier\n    # request that had one (NULL when there is none)\n    \"app_minutes_7d\": \"\"\"\n        SELECT COALESCE(\n            CAST(:app_minutes_7d AS double precision),\n            (SELECT app_minutes_7d\n             FROM scoring_points\n             WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n               AND as_of < :now\n               AND app_minutes_7d IS NOT NULL\n             ORDER BY as_of DESC\n             LIMIT 1))\n    \"\"\",\n    # smoothed churn rate of the customer's postcode district: (s + 20 p) / (n + 20), where p is the mean churned_30d\n    # of all label rows (0.05 when there are none), and n and s are the number of label rows of customers in the same\n    # district and the sum of their churned_30d\n    \"te_district\": \"\"\"\n        WITH lab AS (\n            SELECT l.churned_30d, c.postcode_district\n            FROM labels l\n            JOIN customers c ON c.customer_id = l.customer_id\n        ),\n        own AS (\n            SELECT postcode_district\n            FROM customers\n            WHERE customer_id = :customer_id\n        )\n        SELECT (COALESCE(SUM(lab.churned_30d) FILTER (WHERE lab.postcode_district = own.postcode_district), 0)\n                + 20 * COALESCE(AVG(lab.churned_30d), 0.05))\n               / (COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE lab.postcode_district = own.postcode_district) + 20)\n        FROM lab CROSS JOIN own\n    \"\"\",\n}\n\nFEATURES = list(QUERIES)\n\"\"\"Churn feature pipeline and model for the retention team.\"\"\"\n\"\"\"Customer attributes from the customer record (new in v3): the subscription plan and the days since the last login.\n\nPoint in time: a last login at or after the prediction time is ignored (the feature is null then), so the feature\nnever looks ahead of t.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef plan(points, customers):\n    \"\"\"The customer's subscription plan.\"\"\"\n    return points[\"customer_id\"].map(customers.set_index(\"customer_id\")[\"plan\"])\n\n\ndef days_since_last_login(points, customers):\n    \"\"\"t minus the customer's last login, in days; null when the record has no login before t.\"\"\"\n    last = points[\"customer_id\"].map(customers.set_index(\"customer_id\")[\"last_login_at\"])\n    days = (points[\"as_of\"] - last).dt.total_seconds() / 86400\n    return days.where(last < points[\"as_of\"])\n\"\"\"Build the churn feature table: python -m churn.build_features --data <dir> --out <file.parquet>\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport os\n\nfrom churn.attributes import days_since_last_login, plan\nfrom churn.encoding import district_encoding\nfrom churn.extract import load_extract\nfrom churn.impute import fill_app_minutes\nfrom churn.status import latest_status\nfrom churn.windows import activity_features, tenure_days\n\nFEATURE_COLUMNS = [\"orders_7d\", \"orders_30d\", feats[\"days_since_last_login\"] = days_since_last_login(points, t[\"customers\"])\n    feats[\"app_minutes_7d\"] = fill_app_minutes(points)\n    feats[\"te_district\"] = district_encoding(points, ap.add_argument(\"--data\", required=True, help=\"folder with the extract CSVs\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", required=True, help=\"output parquet file\")\n    a = ap.parse_args()\n    feats = build(a.data)\n    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(a.out)), exist_ok=True)\n    feats.to_parquet(a.out, index=False)\n    print(f\"wrote {len(feats)} rows x {feats.shape[1]} columns to {a.out}\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n\"\"\"Target encoding of high-cardinality categoricals, point in time (INC-2041).\n\nA label row records whether the customer churned in the 30 days after its own prediction, so its outcome is final only\nonce that window has closed (as_of + 30 days). A prediction at t uses only labels whose window closed before t, for the\ndistrict counts and for the prior alike; its own label and every later one are never seen.\n\"\"\"\nimport numpy as np\nimport pandas as pd\n\nPRIOR_WEIGHT = 20\nDEFAULT_PRIOR = 0.05\nLABEL_WINDOW_DAYS = 30\nEPOCH = pd.Timestamp(\"1970-01-01\", tz=\"UTC\")\nSTRIDE = 10 ** 10\n\n\ndef _seconds(ts):\n    return ((ts - EPOCH) // pd.Timedelta(seconds=1)).to_numpy(dtype=\"int64\")\n\n\ndef district_encoding(points, labels, customers, m=PRIOR_WEIGHT):\n    \"\"\"Smoothed churn rate of the customer's postcode district, from labels whose window closed before t.\"\"\"\n    district = customers.set_index(\"customer_id\")[\"postcode_district\"]\n    lab_d = labels[\"customer_id\"].map(district)\n    pt_d = points[\"customer_id\"].map(district)\n    codes, _ = pd.factorize(pd.concat([lab_d, pt_d], ignore_index=True))\n    lab_code, pt_code = codes[:len(labels)].astype(\"int64\"), codes[len(labels):].astype(\"int64\")\n    closed = _seconds(labels[\"as_of\"]) + LABEL_WINDOW_DAYS * 86400\n    y = labels[\"churned_30d\"].to_numpy(dtype=\"int64\")\n    t = _seconds(points[\"as_of\"])\n\n    order = np.argsort(closed, kind=\"stable\")\n    ycum = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(y[order])])\n    N = np.searchsorted(closed[order], t, side=\"left\")\n    S = ycum[N]\n    prior = np.full(len(t), DEFAULT_PRIOR)\n    has = N > 0\n    prior[has] = S[has] / N[has]\n\n    key = lab_code * STRIDE + closed\n    o2 = np.argsort(key, kind=\"stable\")\n    ycum2 = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(y[o2])])\n    hi = np.searchsorted(key[o2], pt_code * STRIDE + t, side=\"left\")\n    lo = np.searchsorted(key[o2], pt_code * STRIDE, side=\"left\")\n    n, s = hi - lo, ycum2[hi] - ycum2[lo]\n    return pd.Series((s + m * prior) / (n + m), index=points.index, dtype=\"float64\")\n\"\"\"Load a warehouse extract (the seven CSV files described in docs/WAREHOUSE.md) into Every column of the\nfeature table other than customer_id and as_of is a model input: numeric columns as they are (a null is a missing\nvalue), string columns one-hot encoded. A column that holds no value at all is skipped.\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport os\n\nimport pandas as pd\nfrom sklearn.ensemble import HistGradientBoostingClassifier\nfrom sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score\n\nKEYS = [\"customer_id\", \"as_of\"]\nLABEL = \"churned_30d\"\nTRAIN_END = pd.Timestamp(\"2025-09-01\", tz=\"UTC\")\nVALID_END = pd.Timestamp(\"2025-12-01\", tz=\"UTC\")\n\n\ndef inputs(feats):\n    return [c for c in feats.columns if c not in KEYS and c != LABEL]\n\n\ndef categories(feats):\n    \"\"\"Sorted values of each string column (one indicator column per value).\"\"\"\n    return {c: sorted(feats[c].dropna().astype(str).unique()) for c in inputs(feats)\n            if not pd.api.types.is_numeric_dtype(feats[c])}\n\n\ndef design_matrix(feats, cats):\n    X = feats[[c for c in inputs(feats) if c not in cats]].astype(\"float64\").copy()\n    for c, values in cats.items():\n        for v in values:\n            X[f\"{c}_{v}\"] = (feats[c] == v).astype(\"float64\")\n    return X\n\n\ndef make_model(l2=1.0, max_depth=4):\n    return HistGradientBoostingClassifier(max_iter=300, learning_rate=0.05, max_depth=max_depth,\n                                          l2_regularization=l2, early_stopping=False, random_state=0)\n\n\ndef train_validate(feats, labels, l2=1.0, max_depth=4):\n    \"\"\"Fit on labelled predictions before TRAIN_END, validate on TRAIN_END .. last_login_days(points, customers, ops):\n    \"\"\"days_since_last_login where the extract determines it, and a mask of the rows where it does not.\n\n    The record holds the CRM's last login as of the start of the latest committed crm_sync; only its current value is\n    kept. The value at t is the current one if it was last overwritten before t, or if the current last login is older\n    than the start of the latest sync committed before t (no login happened in between); otherwise it is unknown.\n    \"\"\"\n    vis, _ = customer_visible(points, customers)\n    c = customers.set_index(\"customer_id\")\n    L = points[\"customer_id\"].map(c[\"last_login_at\"])\n    U = points[\"customer_id\"].map(c[\"_updated_at\"])\n    t = sec(points[\"as_of\"])\n    syncs = ops.crm_syncs()\n    starts = np.array([sec(s) for s, _ in syncs], dtype=\"int64\")\n    commits = np.array([sec(a) for _, a in syncs], dtype=\"int64\")\n    order = np.argsort(commits, kind=\"stable\")\n    starts, commits = starts[order], commits[order]\n    k = np.searchsorted(commits, t, side=\"left\") - A run on `/app/data` takes about 1 second.\n\n**Why the numbers differed.** The v3 pipeline only used events dated before `t`, but the live service only sees rows the warehouse had *committed* before `t`. Some rows dated before `t` were committed later. Some values were overwritten later, and one table changed under the same name. Per feature:\n- **Orders features** (`orders_7d`, `orders_30d`, `spend_30d_cents`, `days_since_last_order`): until 2025-06-01T10:18:55Z, the table called `orders` was a legacy table loaded once a night. It has since been dropped, so the extract only holds its replacement. I rebuild the legacy table from the scheduler log: which date windows each nightly load selected, and whether its transaction committed. The order rows themselves come from the extract. Every window's logged row count matches the extract, and the warehouse's own comparison of the two tables found them equal. The extract determines the value at `t` for only 1,258 of 57,909 predictions. In the January sample, none of the 361 undetermined requests had a value equal to the current one.\n\nIf another brand's extract doesn't determine any other feature for some rows, the command leaves that feature out and says so on stderr.
